  uses these pseudo-elements for fixed bullets, circles and check marks, so removing the capability would break
  valid visual semantics.

  runtime use of either API; the frontend architecture guard now prevents the four S03 applications and shared
  packages from introducing Service Worker registration, OPFS, legacy FileSystem API or Web SQL persistence.
  The separately owned controlled-terminal extension remains outside that application-runtime rule.

- Round 156 closes the cross-navigation `Window.name` DLP gap. Unlike ordinary JavaScript memory,
  `window.name` survives same-tab document navigations and could carry a Cookie, token, OTP, proxy password,
  profile path, complete phone number or other account secret from a prior document. The four applications do not
  use named browsing contexts, but their previous early browser boundary neither cleared this inherited value nor
  prevented a later write.
- The Design System now installs `installClientWindowNameSecurity` before diagnostics, storage projection,
  navigation security and router construction. It clears the browser slot without reading its former contents,
  replaces the configurable browser accessor with a getter that always returns the empty string and a setter that
  discards every write, and never restores prior contents during cleanup. Existing `_blank` links retain
  `noopener noreferrer`; no application behavior depends on a named top-level context.
- The production screenshot boundary and local visual-evidence boundary now reject any nonempty `Window.name`.
  The auto Playwright fixture adds only one fixed-key `window-name-length` count and requires literal zero after

  name contents never enter an attachment, screenshot error, JSON evidence or external audit. The architecture
  guard freezes the early clear/seal, count-only runtime result, screenshot rejection and negative tests.

- Round 155 closes the remaining non-React browser error-reporting DLP path. The React 19 root callbacks added
  in Round 154 cover render and hydration failures, but ordinary event-handler errors and unhandled Promise
  rejections still reached the browser's raw default reporting channel. `installClientBrowserSecurity` now
  installs one early shared `error`/`unhandledrejection` boundary before the router. It emits only the existing
  fixed-kind, allow-listed-name, frame-count and cause-presence projection, then stops the raw browser event.
  It never reads an event message, stack, rejection message, URL, form value or browser store.
- Suppressing the raw default alone also hid the failure from Playwright's `pageerror` count. The final boundary
  therefore relays a newly allocated, fixed-message sentinel through the browser after the original value is
  destroyed. A `WeakSet` recognizes exactly those sentinels and lets their generic browser default proceed.
  This preserves literal-zero runtime enforcement without allowing the original secret-bearing Error or
  rejection value into console, telemetry or test evidence. Uninstall removes both listeners with the rest of
  the shared browser-security boundary.

- Round 154 closes the React 19 error-reporting DLP bypass. The shared `ProductErrorBoundary` previously redacted
  its optional callback, but none of the four application roots installed a receiver; React 19 root hydration
  also had no `onCaughtError`, `onUncaughtError` or `onRecoverableError`. React 19's documented defaults send
  caught render errors to `console.error` and uncaught errors to `window.reportError`, so raw thrown messages
  could bypass the shared projection. The old “错误已按安全规则记录” copy was also stronger than the actual
  no-receiver behavior. The audit found no `sendBeacon`, third-party analytics/error SDK or other application
  telemetry transport.
- The Design System now owns one ephemeral `geo:safe-client-diagnostic` event channel and the three React 19
  root callbacks. Error reports contain only a fixed kind enum, an allow-listed standard error name, a bounded
  component-frame count and a `hasCause` boolean. Raw message, stack lines, cause contents, URL, form values,
  account identifiers and browser state are never copied. A throwing optional receiver cannot turn an already
  handled product failure into a second failure. `ProductErrorBoundary` and validated identity bootstrap use
  the same projection by default, and the UI now truthfully says the error was safely handled rather than
  claiming durable storage. All four `hydrateRoot` calls install the shared callbacks; the architecture guard
  rejects local console/reportError/sendBeacon handlers and any return to raw message/stack inspection.

  network transfer and records the measured decoded byte length, then supplies those decoded bytes to the exact
  production generated-client boundary. All four applications reject the body before OpenAPI parsing, Query
  state or business reads, expose only local retry, retain no canary in DOM/URL/storage/history and remain axe
  clean. The completed transfer also keeps console, page and failed-request counts at literal zero.

  telemetry and error surfaces without duplicating endpoint contracts. Report artifacts retain their existing
  format-specific integrity boundaries, while the media dataset shares the same 25 MiB ceiling.

  The receipt must now be exactly a safe `running` acknowledgement with null timestamps and no source payload.
  An authoritative `never` state must be completely empty, while `running`, `done` and `failed` require valid
  calendar timestamps in nondecreasing order, a nonblank bounded message and exactly all three source records.
  Pending sources must remain zero-row/note-free and a completed record cannot contain a pending source. Invalid
  states fail locally as unavailable and cannot produce a running, failed or completed claim. No S01 execution
  business file or server contract changed.

- Round 142 closes a remaining refresh-status ordering race. The initial or locally retried status read now owns
  a monotonic read generation; starting a refresh invalidates every outstanding read before the write begins, so
  a delayed pre-refresh “尚未刷新” response cannot replace a newer completed result. A failed start attempted
  while the initial status is loading also restores the honest unavailable state rather than leaving a permanent
  loading claim. No S01 execution business file or server contract changed.

  refresh is active. Refresh source names are restricted to the three contract platforms; timestamps, messages
  and notes are bounded and fail closed on normalized token, Cookie, OTP, phone, profile-path or biometric

- Round 111 closes synchronous duplicate submission and project-generation ownership across Customer profile
  declaration, atomic asset confirmation and change requests. React Hook Form/React pending state was not a
  synchronous lock, so two DOM submit events in the same turn could start two generated-client writes with
  different idempotency keys. The shared local `useResourceMutationGuard` now acquires a ref lock before any
  state update, captures the exact tenant/project/source generation and discards a delayed continuation after
  context change or unmount. Explicit pending state keeps each initiating form disabled and accurately announced
  until its one request settles.
- The new hostile three-viewport browser test dispatches two synchronous submit events per form while delaying
  the first response. It proves exactly one profile, asset and question POST, validates the exact captured request
  bodies, preserves normal completion after release and keeps deliberate `token`, `authorization`, `cookie` and

- Round 110 closes a Customer tenant-member governance write race. OIDC bind/revoke, member revoke and invite
  previously had separate UI loading signals, so an OIDC request could remain in flight while the same member was
  removed or another tenant write began. The browser now owns one synchronous tenant-governance mutation lock and
  one monotonically increasing request generation across all four writes. Each continuation captures the
  validated tenant, actor, live/fixture source and retry generation plus the exact initiating member; a
  superseded continuation is discarded, while a completed write updates only that captured member. During an
  active write all member-management triggers, invitation controls and conflicting dialog actions are disabled
  and announced through an accessible status. Closing the dialog does not pretend to cancel an already-sent
  write, and the generated client still validates the response against the requested membership/user before any
  React state is updated.
- The new hostile browser test delays member A's OIDC response, proves removal, member B management and invitation
  are all locked, closes the dialog, releases the response and then verifies only A is shown as bound. It also
  proves the one-time IdP subject plus deliberate `token`/`profile_path` response extensions reach none of DOM,
  URL, local/session storage or history state. The three target viewports pass 3/3 in 27,476 ms; evidence
  `tests/s04-evidence/e2e-results-s03-round110-member-integrity.json` has SHA-256
  `e124f3b6da9bdeeeed0959dc196b811ff2b0f79dbcf67e703bf49e293ea0b508`. The complete existing member

- Round 109 closes two Customer Evidence Workspace resource-generation races. An answer relation request was
  bound to the selected `ans_` ID but not to the answer filter/page URL, so browser history could leave an old
  answer dialog open after the catalog moved to another page. Relation generations now advance and the dialog,
  relation projection and state are cleared whenever project, source, answer cursor/page, filter, query or local
  retry context changes. A delayed prior-page relation cannot reopen or populate the current page. Evidence
  package creation now captures the exact current-page `evd_` IDs and uses a separate generation invalidated by
  asset cursor/page, retry, project or source changes. A delayed successful POST from page one therefore cannot
  display a success receipt on page two, while the immutable request remains correctly addressed to page one's
  assets.

  generated-client boundary and applies the same contract to Customer Web. Both applications now consume
  `loadProjectReportCatalog`, which validates generated report pages in batches of 100, scans at most ten server
  pages, retains only exact current-project summaries and looks ahead for one next matching report. A legitimate
  other-project row is omitted rather than treated as corruption; a mixed tenant page selects and probes only
  the current-project report. If 1,000 safe tenant rows still do not establish the next current-project report,
  Customer Web exposes an accessible continuation page and the number scanned without claiming a project total
  or probing any other report detail. Unsafe project/cursor context fails before any request. The duplicated
  application-local scanner and handwritten interpretation have been removed.

- Round 107 closes Report Studio's tenant-catalog/project-context boundary and cross-report client-state
  isolation. The generated report list contract is intentionally tenant-scoped and exposes no
  `project_pub_id` filter, but the browser previously requested only one row and rejected the result whenever
  that row belonged to another project. A valid current-project report later in the ordered tenant catalog was
  therefore unreachable. The browser now scans at most ten generated-client pages of 100 safe summaries, retains
  only exact current-project rows, looks ahead for the next matching report, keeps the filtered cursor in URL
  history and truthfully exposes an incomplete scan rather than inferring a project total. Cross-project-only
  rows render neither identity nor detail probe. The backend contract remains unchanged; a server-side project
  filter is still desirable for very large tenant catalogs.
- Report detail 401/403/404 now retains the shared non-inferential forbidden state instead of becoming a generic
  failure after a successful catalog read. Review, outcome and detail workspaces are keyed by exact
  `(report_pub_id, report_version_pub_id)` identity, so a direct browser-history change cannot reuse a previous
  report's comments, AI-review state, receipt, workflow state or action ID. Review/publish/revision continuations
  also ignore UI callbacks after their originating workspace unmounts, with Strict Mode effect re-entry kept
  correct. The executable negative switches reports while the same review/outcome section remains mounted and
  proves the subsequent PATCH addresses

- Round 105 closes a cross-content page-history/diff binding defect in Intelligence Web. The authoritative
  page-history and visual-diff routes return every content item in an investigation ordered by content ID and
  version. The workspace previously treated the final two rows of the whole investigation as one page's
  previous/current versions and unconditionally displayed the final diff, so a trailing single-version page
  could borrow another content item's version pair and similarity. Safe projections now retain the public
  `content_pub_id`, choose the most recently captured content item by default, group versions by that identity and
  show a diff only when its content, before-version and after-version exactly match the selected adjacent pair.
  Multiple content items are available through a labelled native selector; a single-version item disables
  “上一版本” and truthfully shows no borrowed diff.

  relations with deterministic Bezier curvature and separate labels, so valid relations no longer overlay one
  another or change identity when response order changes.

  evidence-package workspaces are additionally keyed by case (and package verdict) so local form/receipt state
  cannot be reused under another case identity. The executable race delays a page-two verdict receipt, navigates
  twice through browser history to a page-one `unlikely` verdict and proves it remains `rejected` after the old

  object returned by `/terminal/tasks/{task}/complete` as success, so a cross-task, result-mismatched or

  receipt. `validateTerminalResultView` now requires the exact generated five-field `TerminalResultView`, matches
  `task_pub_id` and `platform_result` to the signed request, requires `state=completed` and accepts only a strict,
  timezone-qualified, possible ISO timestamp. The background removes the task only after this validation and
  exposes merely `{state: submitted}` to the popup.

- The controlled extension previously hard-coded every signed terminal result to `challenge_completed`, despite

  `拒绝本次任务` action, signs the same generated-contract result envelope with `result=rejected`, prevents
  concurrent completion/rejection submissions and clears the task only after an authoritative response. A safely
  recognized expired stored task is removed and announced without rendering its identifiers; malformed storage
  remains silently removed. No new endpoint or handwritten response contract was introduced.

  task is removed without rendering. The background exposes only a `resume` projection; completion rereads the
  same validated task instead of trusting raw session storage. On popup reopen, pairing inputs remain disabled,
  only action/domain/challenge are rendered and the completion control is restored. The popup independently
  revalidates that projection before DOM admission.

- The terminal protocol now verifies the canonical complete signed payload but returns and caches only

  to 4 KiB and projected to eight required fields; task payloads, identifiers, nonces, device labels and
  fingerprints are bounded and validated. Secret-shaped/full-phone device labels are rejected. Responses are
  read as a bounded 64 KiB stream, non-2xx responses become fixed status codes, unknown exceptions collapse to
  `terminal_error`, untrusted runtime senders are rejected, and expired session tasks are removed.

  action/domain/challenge summary and requires a second user gesture for the exact API/target origins. The image
  is never uploaded or stored and the token exists only in popup memory until bind consumes it. The build gate
  now forbids textarea/legacy bundle/token controls, raw server error echo, Cookie/proxy/page-content APIs,

- Round 82 closes a browser-history persistence gap exposed by the Round 81 screenshot boundary. ProductShell
  already sanitized URL query/fragment state on initial render and every `popstate`, but preserved
  `window.history.state` verbatim. A same-origin legacy page, hostile navigation caller or future React Router
  state payload could therefore leave a Cookie/token/OTP/phone/profile value in the active history entry: visual
  evidence would correctly reject it, but the application itself would not remove it.
- The shared design system now projects history state before every `replaceState`. It preserves only bounded
  booleans, finite non-secret numbers, 500-character safe strings, 30-item arrays and 50-entry plain records to a
  depth of four and 50,000 serialized characters. Normalized secret keys, secret-shaped strings, six-digit/phone
  numbers, oversized keys/values/collections, non-plain objects, cycles and serialization failures are dropped;
  an unsafe root becomes `null`. URL and history projection are committed together, so no sanitized URL retains
  the old secret state. Safe React Router `idx`/`usr`-style fields and ordinary nested navigation state remain
  intact.

  and POSIX/Windows/UNC profile-path checks. Only fixed booleans cross the page-evaluation boundary; rejected
  values never enter evidence JSON, diagnostics or errors. Evaluation, serialization, size or collection-bound
  failures reject the screenshot and remove its exact stale path.

- Explicit 401/403/404/503 and local-retry scenarios now use one bounded `synthetic-http` harness. It validates

  Node-side `WeakMap`; and passes only a safe rule ID through the browser binding. The browser-internal response
  remains a successful 200 transport, while a read-only status/`ok` projection gives the generated client the
  intended 4xx/5xx result. Thus Chromium emits no resource error and the application still exercises the real
  forbidden/unavailable classifiers. Counters survive a document transition without local/session storage, and
  no raw URL, response body or diagnostic is retained.

  body-bearing 200 so Playwright records the write and Chromium sees a successful transport; the harness returns
  a bodyless projected 204 to the generated client for publish and PATCH operations. Exact backend/OpenAPI
  contract tests continue to own the wire-level 204 assertion. All former mocked-HTTP console and intercepted-204
  request-failure allowances have been deleted.

- Round 72 closes the complete-E2E runtime-error and failure-evidence DLP gap. The prior 35-file/357-test matrix
  had per-test console/request listeners in only part of the suite and no universal uncaught `pageerror` gate.
  Every Playwright test registration now imports one auto fixture that observes console errors, uncaught page
  errors and transport failures. The collector stores only the three fixed issue kinds and count summaries; it
  never reads or retains console text, exception text or request URLs. The old per-scenario diagnostics were
  independently reduced to the same fixed kinds, and the architecture guard rejects a direct Playwright test
  import, raw `message.text()`/location capture or a non-literal diagnostic push. A Vitest fault injection proves
  that Bearer, OTP and `access_token` canaries in all three failure channels cannot enter the stored issue list or
  summary.
- Playwright failure evidence is now fail-closed for account secrecy: automatic trace and failure screenshots are
  disabled, runtime attachments contain only observed/expected counts, generated output directories are excluded
  from source formatting, and stale application servers remain rejected unless explicitly opted in. The final
  machine report and output directory pass a negative scan for Cookie/session, bearer/access/refresh token, OTP,
  forbidden-role and local-retry canaries; the output directory contains only `.last-run.json`.
- The new gate exposed 24 viewport repetitions of eight historical blind spots. Two nominal Customer flows were
  missing an analytics response fixture and now execute with zero runtime issues. Explicit 401/403/404/503
  fail-closed and local-retry tests now declare exact, bounded mocked-HTTP console counts; any extra or missing
  event still fails. Report Studio's intercepted, bodyless 204 contract responses retain a separately named exact
  four-event Playwright transport allowance because Chromium reports those mock fulfillments as `ERR_ABORTED`
  while `fetch` receives the successful 204. No allowance permits an uncaught page error, raw diagnostic value or
  unapproved test file, and the static guard freezes every allowed callsite and count.

- Round 71 closes the resource-exhaustion and stale-bitmap half of verified PDF preview. Customer Web and Report
  Studio already accepted only generated-client, MIME/size/SHA-256-bound PDF bytes, but PDF.js could parse an
  integrity-valid document with an extreme page count, MediaBox or image and allocate an unbounded canvas. A
  failed retry or page/report transition could also leave the previous canvas pixels visible while status moved
  to loading/failed.
- The design system now owns one PDF.js policy and viewport projection. Verified documents are limited to 500
  pages; the requested page must be in range; a rendered canvas is limited to 4,096 pixels per side and 8,388,608
  total pixels; decoded images are capped at 16,777,216 pixels. PDF.js runs with malformed-data recovery,
  worker resource fetches, streaming prefetch, XFA, system fonts and dynamic font-face loading disabled. Customer
  and Report Studio both project `document.numPages` plus the scaled viewport before setting canvas dimensions,
  cancel the active render task and destroy the loading task on cleanup, and clear the canvas to 0×0 on load,
  failure and cancellation. Direct tests cover a valid A4 viewport, invalid page counts/numbers, non-finite/
  oversized dimensions, an over-pixel viewport, the PDF.js options and bitmap clearing. Design-system coverage
  rises from 31/31 to 32/32; the frontend contract guard freezes the shared limits/options and both consumers.
- A shared E2E fixture builds a valid, independently hashed one-page PDF with a 20,000×20,000 MediaBox. Customer
  and Report Studio accept its byte integrity but reject the projected viewport before canvas allocation at
  1600×1100, 1024×768 and 390×844. All six cases keep the canvas at exactly 0×0, expose no rendered-success
  status, make zero external tracker requests, remain WCAG-AA clean and record no console error or failed
  request. The final focused machine report passes 6/6 in 35,320 ms on freshly built ports 64601–64604 with
  `reuseExistingServer=false`:

- Round 70 closes an integrity-valid HTML artifact's pre-parse active-content/resource-load gap. Customer Web
  already fetched report bytes through the generated, MIME/size/SHA-256-bound client and reconstructed only
  allow-listed semantic React nodes, but it passed the verified source to `DOMParser` before rejecting `img`,
  `iframe`, active tags and loading attributes. A hostile but correctly hashed artifact could therefore ask the
  browser parser to resolve an external resource before the later projection failed closed.
- `projectSafeHtmlDocument` now applies a conservative lexical gate before constructing `DOMParser`. Active/
  embedded/form/media tags and event/resource-bearing attributes (`src`, `srcset`, `srcdoc`, `style`,
  `formaction`, `poster`, `xlink:href`, and related forms) reject the complete document before parsing. Safe
  semantic markup remains reconstructed without raw HTML or source attributes, and verified external evidence
  links now carry `rel="noopener noreferrer"`. Direct coverage spies on `DOMParser.parseFromString` and proves it
  is never called for script, event handler, image, iframe/srcdoc, SVG/xlink, remote CSS or form/input documents;
  dangerous link protocols and entity-encoded secrets still fail in the parsed allow-list/DLP boundary.
  Design-system coverage remains 31/31 and the frontend contract guard freezes the pre-parse gate, parser
  non-entry proof, secure-link relation and browser request canary.
- The renderer audit confirms Report Studio consumes only integrity-verified PDF bytes through PDF.js, Customer
  PDF follows the same byte-bound path, Customer HTML is the sole semantic HTML consumer, and Evidence Viewer
  constructs React text/geometry rather than raw markup or remote media. No second HTML/PDF renderer or
  `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` path was found. The existing Customer live HTML screenshot baseline passes unchanged
  at 1600×1100, 1024×768 and 390×844.

- Round 69 closes a legacy/same-origin browser-storage residue gap in the shared identity bootstrap. S03-owned
  application sources do not write business data to local/session storage, but bootstrap previously inspected
  and removed only the three exact `geo.session.*` hints. Secret-bearing values left by an older build, extension
  or same-origin page could therefore remain observable even though current API projections never persisted
  them. This was especially relevant after Rounds 67–68 added normalized value/key detection without applying
  it to pre-existing storage entries.
- `@geo/auth` now scrubs both local and session storage before reading or sending any browser-controlled identity
  hint. It snapshots at most 10,000 entries, removes entries whose normalized key or value is secret-shaped and
  clears a store that exceeds the bound. Safe identity hints and unrelated bounded preferences are retained.
  If a required identity hint is removed or local storage exceeds the limit, contract-fixture mode fails closed
  before an identity request and cannot silently fall back to a fixture. Production still discards all
  browser-controlled identity hints and relies exclusively on the provider-owned cookie/session contract. Unit
  coverage uses a standards-correct enumerable Storage mock and proves full-width/zero-width keys, a full-width
  phone value, a Windows profile value, safe-preference preservation and oversized-store fail-closed behavior.
  Auth coverage rises from 7/7 to 8/8 and the frontend contract guard freezes the scrub, limit and tests.

  but `redactClientDiagnostic`, URL parameter names, browser-generated JSON/CSV keys and API generic structured

  ordinary-looking opaque value.
- The design system now owns `containsClientSecretKey`, applying the same bounded NFKC, zero-width removal and
  three-round percent-decoding normalization before the key allow/deny decision. URL/history sanitation,
  recursive telemetry/error redaction and generated JSON/CSV download validation all use it. The API client has
  the corresponding pre-state `containsBrowserSecretKey` boundary; a secret-shaped key makes the complete
  structured record fail closed rather than retaining an ambiguous partial payload. Direct coverage proves
  rejection of full-width `ａｃｃｅｓｓ＿ｔｏｋｅｎ`/`ｔｏｋｅｎ`, zero-width `token`, double-encoded
  `profile_path`, full-width `Cookie`, JSON keys, CSV headers and three independent report-component payloads.
  Safe `metric_version` remains accepted. API-client coverage remains 36/36 and design-system coverage rises to
  31/31; the frontend contract guard freezes both implementations and all unit/E2E canaries.

- Round 67 closes an obfuscated-value gap in the browser DLP boundary. The API projection and shared UI
  detector already rejected labelled secrets, contiguous mainland phone numbers, bare six-digit OTPs and common

  the same values with NFKC/full-width characters, zero-width format characters, repeated percent encoding,
  spaces or hyphens inside a phone/OTP, or `profile_dir`/Windows/UNC browser-profile notation.
- `packages/api-client` and `packages/design-system` now normalize each candidate with NFKC, remove the bounded
  zero-width set and decode percent encoding at most three rounds before applying the same expanded value
  patterns. Both boundaries reject separated phone and OTP forms, encoded bearer/session values and POSIX,
  Windows or UNC profile paths. The API client applies this before its generated-contract projection resolves
  into application/Query state; the design system reuses it for context, navigation, URL/history, diagnostics,
  structured renderers and downloads. Safe version/date/count text remains accepted. Direct tests exercise each
  hostile form independently, including zero-width and full-width phones, `824-911`,
  `Bearer%2520encoded-session-canary`, `profile_dir=C:\...User Data\Profile 1` and a UNC profile path.
  API-client coverage rises to 36/36 and design-system coverage to 30/30. The frontend contract guard freezes
  normalization, both package boundaries, tests and the shared-shell network fixture.

  `section` allow-list, so `?section=execution` selected no button while the application rendered Overview.
- `projectSafeProductNavigation` now bounds the navigation to 32 unique items, accepts only 64-character
  identifier-shaped IDs, 60-character non-secret/control-free labels and optional 12-character safe badges.
  Link destinations must be a canonical relative `/platform/...` path no longer than 300 characters, without
  origin, credentials, traversal normalization, query or fragment. Secret labels/badges and duplicate/invalid
  IDs disappear atomically. An otherwise safe item with an invalid destination remains discoverable as a
  disabled button labelled “导航地址未通过安全校验”, never as a clickable or secret-bearing href; shared styling
  makes that failure state visibly disabled on every viewport.
- `ProductShell` derives its `section` fallback and allow-list only from projected non-link, non-disabled items.
  Safe internal href items remain real links and cannot be selected through query state. Direct projection
  coverage proves safe unique output, the exact Operations deep link and removal/disablement of query-bearing,
  external-origin, secret-label, secret-badge and duplicate inputs. Mounted component coverage proves
  `?section=execution` is removed, Overview becomes current, the safe execution link remains and no hostile
  navigation value reaches DOM/URL. Design-system coverage rises to 29/29; the contract guard freezes the
  projection, section/href separation, disabled failure state and Operations deep-link evidence.

- Round 65 closes the full-URL/browser-history portion of the DLP and long-URL gate. The shared sanitizer
  previously removed secret-shaped query keys/values and invalid `section` values, but left `#fragment`
  untouched and retained arbitrarily many or long parameters when their contents did not look like a secret.
  A token/OTP could therefore remain in fragment/history, and a benign-shaped multi-kilobyte parameter could
  survive every navigation. Customer's “前往报告/前往监测导出” helper also wrote `history.pushState` directly
  instead of entering the shared URL boundary.
- `sanitizeClientUrl` now decodes each query key/value and fragment up to three times before DLP inspection,
  removing nested-encoded secrets without decoding content into the rendered page. It bounds query state to 32
  entries, 80-character names, 500-character values and a 4,096-character complete URL; fragments are bounded to
  512 characters and removed when secret-shaped. Oversized safe parameters are deterministically removed from
  the end while retaining the allow-listed section and earliest bounded filter state when possible. Secret/
  malformed cursors still atomically remove their coupled page. Safe public fragments and short unknown query
  state remain available instead of being globally discarded.
- The design system now owns `navigateClientSection`, which validates the requested section, sanitizes the
  current complete URL before pushing history and emits the shared popstate transition. Customer's two cross-
  workspace actions use it. The frontend contract guard now rejects direct browser-history mutations anywhere in
  S03-owned application source and freezes the fragment/length/Customer integration tests; no owned app directly
  calls `pushState` or `replaceState`. Design-system coverage rises to 27/27 and proves multi-encoded fragment
  removal, safe fragment retention, parameter name/value/count/total limits, history-state preservation,
  allow-listed navigation and rejection of an unsafe section.

- The design system now owns `downloadSafeGeneratedFile`, explicitly separate from the cryptographically verified
  server-Blob path. It accepts only ASCII DLP-safe `.json` or `.csv` filenames, JSON/CSV encoded as UTF-8 and a

  5,000 array items, 500 fields per object) with bounded non-secret keys/strings and no numeric OTP/phone shape.
  CSV requires a bounded header/line structure, rejects secret-shaped headers/content and rejects cells beginning
  with spreadsheet formula sigils. A valid file alone receives a short-lived object URL, attached `noopener`
  download anchor and delayed URL revocation.

  region. Manual review of the first mobile baseline found that the generic 520px table minimum unnecessarily

- Round 60 closes the numeric form of the generated-client structured-data DLP gap. Free-form Report component,
  frozen-fact, event, filter, optimization and retest records projected finite JSON numbers without checking
  whether an integer was a six-digit OTP or complete mainland mobile number. The shared UI's recursive

- Round 52 closes a false-empty boundary in Customer analytics monitoring. The generated `/analytics/delta`
  response remains an unrestricted object; the former browser boundary silently converted a malformed supported
  metric to three `null` values and returned no completeness facts, so a wholly invalid response could be
  presented as an ordinary empty comparison. `getAnalyticsDelta` now returns an
  `AnalyticsDeltaProjection` derived from the generated response, reconstructs only the four supported metric
  keys, accepts only explicit `null` or bounded finite numbers, drops invalid recognized metrics, and preserves
  exact `total`, `shown` and `invalid` facts. Unknown root/nested extensions, Cookie, Bearer, OTP, proxy-password
  and profile-path values cannot cross the client boundary.
- Customer Web consumes that projection metadata before React rendering. Window delta and confirmed competitors
  now have independent loading/forbidden/failed/ready state, so an invalid delta cannot erase a valid competitor
  visualization. A partial delta remains visibly labelled `窗口差值` in the accessible incomplete-projection
  warning; a non-empty response with zero safe supported metrics becomes a local-retry failure rather than
  `暂无数据`. The contract guard forbids the former raw `ProjectResourceResult<AnalyticsDeltaResponse>` and freezes
  the projection alias, metadata consumer, independent local states and browser negative.
- API client remains 33/33 and Customer remains 25/25. The API negative injects one valid and one malformed
  supported metric plus secret extensions and proves the exact 2→1 projection is secret-free. The browser
  negative proves a wholly invalid delta fails only its own panel, keeps the competitor panel usable and leaks

  monotonic creation/update times. Displayed retests require unique ordered `rts_` identities, exact enclosing
  action binding, safe `usr_` recorder, secret-free bounded result, monotonic measurement/creation times and the
  backend's measurement/ID order. A cross-action row or a result containing a secret is discarded, marks the
  projection incomplete and locks action/retest writes instead of being sorted into a plausible history.

  carrying a hostile/malformed receipt stops after the retest POST, emits no completion PATCH and exposes no
  response secret in DOM, URL, local/session storage or captured request bodies. The normal generated-contract

  routes. S04 production evidence records 21/21 authenticated browser checks, Customer profile/assets at desktop,
  tablet and mobile, zero console/page/request/HTTP failures and no emitted secrets. The older developer process at
  `127.0.0.1:45200` remains on 71 paths but is no longer the qualification target for these routes.
- Mounted the Customer brand/product workspace on real brand/competitor catalog reads and the new atomic
  asset-confirmation contract. Only safe resource IDs, names, credential-free HTTPS websites and projected
  confirmation fields enter React state; unknown data and hostile Cookie/token/OTP/profile extensions are
  discarded. Product/service plus prohibited claim now persist in the same versioned transaction instead of being
  split into partially successful mutations. Three-viewport visual/DLP cases pass.
- Mounted Customer `query-items` and `goals` reads in the questions/targets workspace. The open resource payload is
  reduced to safe IDs, question text/integer priority and allow-listed metric/target/state fields before React
  state; nested Cookie/OTP/profile extensions are dropped. The existing generated `change-requests` write remains
  the only mutation, preserving the rule that a customer request does not directly change scheduling truth.
- Removed the remaining live Monitoring fixture leakage. The mounted analytics delta and competitor endpoints now
  drive period comparison and confirmed-competitor charts through generated clients and strict projections.
  Model breakdown, daily trend, region × mode and question-row sections render `insufficient` until typed facts are
  available. A forbidden overview now exits at workspace level, so it cannot reveal fixture KPIs or duplicate
  resource-existence signals.
- Regenerated the current 71-path OpenAPI after the shared API introduced optional legacy-session cookie auth.
  Browser callers still require a `NonNullable` generated identity-header projection and never read or persist that
  cookie. The generated manifest was synchronized and `pnpm check:api` passes.
- Current validation after integration:
  - generated API client 13/13, including the new confirmation cursor/idempotency paths;
  - complete frontend unit task graph 17/17 and Python 99/99;
  - Ruff, strict TypeScript, mypy (87 files), OpenAPI drift and frontend architecture guards pass;
  - sequential production SPA builds pass for all four applications;
  - final cursor/live focused matrices pass 9/9 and 27/27;
  - Customer mounted-product live/DLP/non-inference/cursor matrix passes 15/15 at 1600×1100, 1024×768 and 390×844;
  - the clean complete Playwright matrix passes 216/216 in 3.5 minutes after report/case/evidence/declaration/asset
    history pagination and live-truthfulness changes, with zero failed or skipped scenarios.
- Re-probed the running real API at `127.0.0.1:45200`: health and the 71-path OpenAPI return 200. With the existing
  safe S04 admin membership, analytics overview/answers, evidence assets, reports and investigations all return
  HTTP 200 and honest empty pages; no secret or service token was used. Empty production facts are not promoted
  into fabricated non-empty UI.
- Re-probed health and OpenAPI after the final 216/216 rerun: both return HTTP 200 and the live schema remains at
  71 paths. This confirms the regenerated client matches the currently mounted service, but does not supply the
  still-missing detail contracts or an authorized external-platform qualification account.
- Re-audited the live schema before the 216/216 browser run: `/api/v2/health` and OpenAPI still return 200
  with 71 paths. The schema has no screenshot-anchor/history-diff/preview path and still lacks the typed detail

- Added 33 live-contract visual baselines: Customer home/assets/questions/monitoring/evidence/report, Report published review/outcome
  and Intelligence graph/verdict/package at desktop, tablet and mobile. Each live test also asserts root
  containment. The full versioned baseline set is now 123 images.
- Rechecked the live and frozen OpenAPI after S01's latest delivery: the schema grew from 36 to 44 paths and now
  contains the customer-safe platform-account list/registration, authorization, pairing list/create, event list
  and revocation workflow endpoints.
- Regenerated the customer boundary from OpenAPI path types and added generated-client wrappers for all seven
  customer account operations. HTTP 401/403/404 all fail closed to the same forbidden presentation so an
  unauthorized role cannot infer account existence.
- Replaced the Customer account fixture for validated live sessions. The live branch now registers an account,
  creates its authorization, creates and polls a controlled-terminal pairing, projects safe event summaries and
  starts revocation without inventing a completion receipt. Development sessions remain visibly labelled fixtures.
- Added a hostile-response DLP E2E in which otherwise successful account/event responses include Cookie, token,
  OTP, full-phone and profile-path fields. Only the allow-listed safe projection reaches React state; no canary
  reaches DOM, storage, URL or captured writes/diagnostics.
- Verified the S01 customer router directly against FastAPI/Postgres: 3/3 integration tests passed. The generated
  API client has 7/7 tests, Customer Testing Library has 9/9, and the complete frontend unit suite has 17/17.
- Updated the nine Customer account/pairing/native-challenge screenshots at 1600×1100, 1024×768 and 390×844.
  The customer account Playwright suite passes 21/21 across those viewports. The first full run reached 186/189;
  its only failures were one obsolete ambiguous test locator repeated at three viewports. After narrowing the
  locator, the complete production-browser suite passes 189/189.
- Added an opt-in `GEO_VITE_NO_WATCH=1` build mode to all four applications because the shared host has exhausted
  its inotify instance quota. Sequential production builds pass without modifying host sysctls or other processes.
- Fully read the consolidated plan, V2 implementation plan, implementation status, comparison report, S00 evidence,
  ownership, frozen conventions/ADRs/OpenAPI, client workspace design and the complete account/session custody plan.
- Audited all four React applications and S03-owned shared packages. Baseline was four static health-probe welcome
  pages; the frozen OpenAPI currently has only health/readiness, project listing and collection-run creation.
- Replaced the welcome page with a shared responsive product shell, semantic navigation, skip link, project context,
  action header, metric cards, table/card patterns, badges and unified product status panels.
- Implemented distinct `loading`, `empty`, `real-zero`, `insufficient`, `failed`, `delayed`, `forbidden` and `ready`
  presentation semantics with local retry support.
- Added a narrow `AccountSummaryProjection` and `AccountSummary` that expose only masked account and lifecycle
  summaries, explicitly prohibiting secrets. `adapter_ready` is labelled “未经 live 验证”.
- Exported `AuthorizationScope`, `CustodyMode`, `SessionHealth`, `AdmissionLevel`, `InterventionStatus` and
  `RevocationReceipt`. Added an allow-list projection boundary that discards all unknown API properties before
  account data reaches UI/cache/URL/telemetry.
- Added the interactive Customer account lifecycle:
  registration summary → custody mode → one-time limited pairing → second confirmation → native-platform
  challenge → complete/refuse/timeout → health result → revocation receipt. It never asks for OTP, Cookie or token.
- Installed the fixed-stack frontend dependencies that were absent from the S00 skeleton: TanStack Query/Table,
  React Hook Form, Zod/resolvers, ECharts, React Flow, PDF.js, Konva/react-konva, Testing Library and Playwright.
- Added browser-DOM interaction coverage for pairing completion, refusal, timeout and revocation, including checks
  that no secret input exists and no DLP canary reaches DOM, URL or browser storage.
- Added Playwright infrastructure and verified the complete account pairing→verification→revocation flow at
  1600×1100, 1024×768 and 390×844. Console errors and failed requests are asserted to be zero.
- The first tablet run found hidden navigation labels also removed accessible names. Added explicit nav
  `aria-label`; the three-viewport rerun passed.
- Added shared `ExperienceProvider` with tenant/project/user/role context, one TanStack Query client per application,
  consistent retry/staleness defaults and a product Error Boundary.
- Added recursive client-diagnostic redaction before telemetry/error reporting; secret-named keys and secret-looking
  values are removed while safe request context remains.
- Wrapped all four application roots in role-appropriate experience contexts.
- Customer monitoring now uses TanStack Table and React Router `useSearchParams` for allow-listed time-window,
  model and answer-mode filters. Browser history restores each filter state.
- Replaced Customer profile, brand/product/competitor and question/goal/change-request placeholders with real
  React Hook Form + Zod workflows:
  - profile truth confirmation, version generation and separated customer/AI/history provenance;
  - HTTPS official-site validation and customer-confirmed brand/product/competitor registration;
  - question priority, metric target and pause/resume/backfill/add-query requests that enter review rather than
    mutating scheduling truth.
- Added responsive form, validation, history, asset-list and request-queue patterns. Visual review found full-page
  screenshot stitching exposed the negative-offset skip link; switched to standard clipped hiding with focus reveal.
- Replaced the remaining Customer placeholders:
  - project-stage home, single recommended next action, latest KPIs and honest delayed-data state;
  - paginated AI answers with model/mode/region/question URL filters, sources, answer screenshot, bbox/text anchor,
    history diff and downloadable secret-free evidence-package manifest;
  - published-report center with version-bound questions, receipt confirmation, historical versions and real
    filtered CSV download; unavailable PDF remains disabled instead of fabricating an artifact;
  - customer member invitation/role management with masked email projections.
- Reworked Playwright into app-specific Customer and Operations projects at all three viewports. The root
  `pnpm test:e2e` now starts both correct application servers and no longer routes S01 tests through Customer.
- Established vertical application experiences:
  - Customer: KPI/model/question views and platform-account authorization/custody/pairing/revocation entry.
  - Operations: overall health, human intervention, authorization/lease, quarantine and revocation states without

  - Report Studio: frozen window, KPI trace, section provenance and review-oriented workspace.

- Completed the Report Studio vertical workflow: immutable data-window freeze, KPI contribution trace, AI/human
  chapter provenance, accessible Konva evidence annotation, paginated preview, comments/release gates, publication
  state and optimization-effect review.
- Switched Playwright web servers from cold Vite development transforms to production builds served by Vite
  preview. This removed the first-viewport `504 Outdated Optimize Dep` race while keeping failed-request assertions.
- Completed the Intelligence vertical workflow: case queue, atomic Claim matrix, source independence and same-origin
  clustering, React Flow propagation view with an equivalent accessible table, immutable page/visual history,
  probability interval/rule explanation, human verdict, version-preserving appeal and safe evidence manifest.
- Replaced the report preview placeholder with real lazy-loaded PDF.js parsing, worker output and canvas rendering;
  the equivalent semantic report copy remains available to assistive technology.
- Added WCAG 2 A/AA axe coverage for every application at all three target viewports. Fixed document titles,
  progress semantics, mobile scroll-region keyboard access, React Flow hidden-focus behavior and attribution
  contrast. The complete accessibility matrix passes 12/12.
- Added the visual data-state matrix for normal/loading/empty/real-zero/insufficient/failed/delayed/forbidden,
  including local retry behavior. It passes axe and screenshots at all three viewports.
- Implemented the previously type-only shared packages:
  - `@geo/charts`: lazy ECharts bar rendering plus an equivalent accessible table;
  - `@geo/evidence-viewer`: reusable screenshot/bbox/text-anchor/history-diff view;
  - `@geo/workflow-ui`: semantic workflow timeline.
    Customer monitoring/evidence and Report outcomes now consume these packages.
- Replaced the handwritten health request with an `openapi-fetch<paths>` generated-path client, regenerated the
  36-path schema/manifest and restored `pnpm check:api`.
- Added browser DLP negative coverage where the API deliberately returns Cookie/token/OTP/proxy password/full
  phone/profile path/biometric canaries; none reaches DOM text, storage, URL or captured diagnostics.
- Added high-load browser coverage for a 120-dimension ECharts view, 500-row table, long evidence text and a hostile
  multi-kilobyte URL. The URL is promptly allow-listed/redacted and all three viewports complete below the 10-second
  ceiling with zero runtime failures.
- Established and then independently rechecked 15 pixel baselines across the four applications at 1600×1100,
  1024×768 and 390×844. Every visual case also asserts the root document has no horizontal overflow.
- Completed the evidence dialog's keyboard contract: focus enters the close control, Tab/Shift+Tab remain trapped,
  Escape closes, and focus returns to the exact trigger. Corrected the neutral badge foreground to pass AA contrast.
- Re-ran the complete production-browser suite after the visual and focus changes: all 54/54 scenarios passed across
  the three target viewports, including runtime failure interception, DLP, large-data and visual comparisons.
- Replaced the Customer account authorization summary-only fixture with an editable RHF/Zod registration workflow:
  account owner, operational responsible person, read/query/draft/publish scopes, expiry, region and custody mode.
  Its allow-listed result updates the shared safe projection and is carried into the pairing confirmation; the form
  contains no password, OTP, Cookie or token field.
- Split the Operations non-execution shell into dedicated overview, session-health, human-intervention and lifecycle
  event views. They cover authorization expiry, leases, native challenges, quarantine, revocation events and a safe
  revocation receipt while leaving S01's execution feature and API calls untouched.
- Completed the Customer monitoring dimension audit: added an allow-listed URL-bound region filter, five-freeze-day
  trend, customer-confirmed competitor comparison and region × answer-mode table. Every chart retains the equivalent
  accessible data table and the region filter participates in browser-history restoration.
- Expanded Report Studio visual regression from its default data-window page to all seven workspaces: freeze,
  KPI trace, chapter editor, Konva evidence editor, real PDF.js preview, review/publish and outcome review at all
  three target viewports. The expansion exposed and fixed a real mobile root-overflow defect by allowing the evidence
  grid item to shrink while keeping the fixed-coordinate canvas inside its local scroll container.
- Expanded Intelligence visual regression to all seven workspaces at all three viewports: cases, Claim matrix,
  source clusters, React Flow propagation plus its table alternative, visual history, verdict/appeal and evidence
  package. All views assert root containment after their async visualization is ready.
- Expanded Operations visual regression to overview, safe session health, human intervention and lifecycle events
  at all three viewports; S01 execution remains separately exercised against its real APIs and was not modified.
- Expanded Customer visual regression to all nine planned workspaces at all three viewports: home, profile,
  brand/product/competitor, questions/goals/change requests, monitoring, answers/evidence, reports, members and
  account authorization/pairing. Each baseline waits for its semantic content (and async charts where relevant) and
  asserts no root-level horizontal overflow.
- Expanded axe from representative pages to every one of the 27 planned workspaces across all three viewports
  (plus the Customer evidence dialog). This found two selector-specific Badge contrast regressions in Customer asset
  and member rows and a non-focusable horizontally scrollable Konva region on tablet/mobile. Badge selectors now
  preserve tone colors, and the evidence canvas is keyboard focusable and scrollable.
- Added a generated-contract identity boundary to `@geo/api-client`: it validates `/identity/session` before
  requesting `/projects`, returns a uniform forbidden result for 401/403, and never sends a service token for a
  human browser session. Session, project and header types all come directly from generated OpenAPI paths.
- Wired that generated identity/project boundary into all four application roots through
  `ValidatedExperienceProvider`. Only allow-listed, non-secret tenant/actor/role runtime hints may initiate the
  request; partial or secret-looking hints are purged and fail closed. Explicit unavailable/forbidden responses
  never fall back, while the no-hint development path remains visibly identified as `contract-fixture`.
- Added three-viewport role-isolation browser coverage: unauthorized sessions render the same forbidden surface
  before a project probe, a validated session adopts the live project context, and a full-phone runtime canary is
  purged before the identity request. All 9/9 cases pass.
- Probed the available real API at `127.0.0.1:45200` using safe S01 E2E tenant/actor/admin headers:
  `/api/v2/identity/session` returned the generated admin session projection and `/api/v2/projects?limit=1`
  returned `S01 E2E Project`, both HTTP 200. No Cookie, bearer token, OTP or service token was supplied.
- Hardened the shared identity projection against secret-shaped values hidden inside otherwise valid generated
  fields. Unsafe tenant/user identifiers fail closed; unsafe project names become a neutral `未命名项目`; unknown
  response properties are never copied. This closes the path from a hostile successful identity/project response
  into application context, DOM, browser storage or URL.
- Added the complete proposed Konva decision record at
  `docs/contract-gaps/S03-ADR-0005-konva-evidence-annotation.md`. It records context, decision, consequences,
  rejected Fabric.js alternative, accessibility/security constraints, validation and S04 promotion instructions
  without mutating the S00-owned accepted ADR directory.
- Promoted pagination, modal dialog and toast from application-specific markup/styles into reusable design-system
  components. Customer evidence and account authorization now consume them. The shared dialog owns modal
  semantics, initial focus, focus trap, Escape/backdrop close and exact trigger-focus restoration.
- Added reusable `FormField`, `FilterBar` and keyboard-focusable `TableRegion` design-system primitives and
  replaced Customer's local form wrapper plus monitoring/evidence filter bars and responsive region × mode table
  wrapper. React Hook Form remains application-owned while labels, hints and error presentation stay consistent.
- Fixed filtered-answer pagination when a URL points at a page beyond the newly filtered result set. Rendering and
  controls now use the bounded page, and a replace navigation removes the stale `answer_page` value without adding
  a misleading history entry.
- Consolidated secret-shaped value detection into the design system and reused it for diagnostic/error redaction,
  safe account summaries, auth/session hints and Customer URL search. Secret values hidden inside otherwise
  allow-listed `ownerLabel`, account mask, expiry, verification, project or generic diagnostic message fields now
  fail closed or become neutral labels rather than reaching UI, cache, URL or telemetry.
- Added a cross-application product-role isolation matrix. Customer rejects operator, Operations rejects customer,
  Report Studio rejects operator and Intelligence rejects customer before any product shell or account lifecycle
  request renders. The common test also asserts project/account fixture labels remain absent and no
  platform-account/intervention/lease request occurs.
- Added direct shared Error Boundary tests. A real render exception containing a full phone, OTP and browser profile
  path produces only a redacted diagnostic, shows the safe fallback and recovers after explicit user retry.
  `StatePanel` also has a direct local-retry assertion.
- Added three-viewport browser recovery for an explicit identity-session 503: no fixture fallback or business
  content appears, the secret-bearing error body reaches no browser surface, and user retry reloads the boundary
  and adopts the successful live project context. Removed a duplicate outer `role=alert` found by the test so
  assistive technology receives one failure announcement.
- Extended diagnostic DLP to numeric values. Six-digit integer challenge codes and integer mainland-phone shapes
  are redacted even when hidden under generic object keys, while ordinary HTTP status and decimal metric values
  remain usable. This closes the JSON-number variant of the OTP/full-phone error-report leak.

## Risks

- Customer-role member administration still lacks role update. Tenant-admin list/create/revoke and the
  OIDC bind/revoke lifecycle and customer-safe responsible-member selector are integrated; unauthorized customer
  sessions continue to receive the same non-inference surface.
- Answer/citation relations, linked screenshots, anchor counts and adjacent evidence-history diff metadata are
  integrated. The current qualified production runtime contains 146 migrated answers and the latest admin-session
  Monitoring acceptance passes, but no real customer-role production identity is verified; customer-role
  populated-data acceptance therefore remains open rather than being inferred from admin access.
- Typed report detail, component versions, artifacts/previews, comment history and optimization-action/retest
  contracts plus delivery creation/recipient confirmation are integrated. Existing version-level evidence
  bindings are projected. Immutable section/component revisions and explicit component-scoped bindings now write
  through the generated client, keep reviewers read-only and preserve AI/human provenance. Production still has
  zero report rows, so real-customer authoring/delivery acceptance is not inferred from mechanism evidence.
- Intelligence detail, immutable page history, adjacent-version visual/text diff metadata, score explanations,
  verdicts and appeals are integrated. The generic evidence-package write accepts the investigation's safe
  evidence IDs, but a server-projected investigation-bound package manifest remains absent.
- No live external account is available. Admission must remain at or below its evidenced level.
- The workspace `.git` directory lacks repository metadata; Turbo cannot derive a Git dirty hash.
- Current generated-contract, frontend architecture, S03 TypeScript/Ruff, unit/integration, production-build and
  browser guards pass. The repository-wide command additionally sees the concurrent S01 formatting/Mypy findings
  recorded above. Production qualification still cannot manufacture missing customer-owned external-platform
  authority or genuine answer/report/investigation rows.

## Next action

- Keep periodically requalifying production OpenAPI, identity roles, S01's composable lifecycle boundary and
  genuine in-scope report/investigation/account samples while continuing owned browser-DLP, artifact-retention
  and responsive/accessibility audits. Integrate any new fields/endpoints in `S03-001` immediately. Final account
  capability qualification additionally requires an approved customer terminal and external-platform target.
  Keep the goal active until the seven completion gates are all evidenced.

## Current external-condition audit — round 1 (2026-07-25 07:02)

- Repository and production OpenAPI each expose 81 paths. The generated schema/client drift guard passes; every
  currently mounted S03-consumed family is integrated.
- Identity exposes member list/create/revoke but no role-update operation. Customer live role mutation therefore
  remains disabled instead of being emulated as a local success or a non-atomic revoke/recreate.
- Report detail exposes version-level evidence bindings, but no component/fact target on the binding and no write

## Post-resume audit — round 19 (2026-07-25 19:59)

- A browser-history audit found that the ordinary Intelligence case catalog and Report catalog already bound
  pagination to the URL, but Anti-GEO calibration kept the dataset, evaluation-run and model-admission cursors in
  local React state. Refresh, shared links and browser back/forward therefore lost the selected page. A malformed
  response could also advertise `has_more` without a valid public cursor and leave a misleading next-page action.
- All three independent calibration collections now use bounded URL page/cursor pairs:
  `cal_dataset_page`/`cal_dataset_cursor`, `cal_run_page`/`cal_run_cursor` and
  `cal_admission_page`/`cal_admission_cursor`. Navigation pushes browser history, reload restores the page, and
  back/forward restores the prior collection state without disturbing its neighbors. Canonicalization uses
  replace only to remove malformed or unpaired values. Cursors require the collection-specific public-ID prefix,
  bounded length and the existing DLP projection; secret-shaped or wrong-family values fail closed before a
  request or React state. `has_more` exposes next only when the corresponding response cursor passes the same
  projection.
- The new executable flow verifies safe page-two navigation, reload, back/forward and malicious request/response
  cursors at all three target viewports. It passes 12/12 in 9,737 ms with WCAG/DLP/browser-storage/runtime checks:

## Post-resume audit — round 22 (2026-07-25 21:11)

- The Report Studio detail audit found two independent real-integration defects. First, the report service
  actually generates `rptv_…` version IDs, while the revision-receipt safety projector and Customer report
  history accepted only fixture-era `rpv_…`; a real revision therefore became “unavailable” after a successful
  write and real customer version rows could disappear. The generated-client boundary now has one shared
  `isReportVersionPubId` guard that accepts the actual `rptv_` family while retaining deployed legacy `rpv_`
  compatibility, and both applications use it. Contract mocks now exercise `rptv_` IDs end to end.
- Second, every report version, fact, component, evidence binding, comment, optimization action and effect retest
  was projected without a browser bound. Report Studio now retains only the latest two versions needed for an
  immutable diff, 500 facts, 100 sections, 100 evidence IDs per section, four unique artifacts, 500 evidence
  bindings/comments, 200 actions and 200 retests per action. Selection retains the highest version numbers and
  the latest chronological action/retest facts without copying/reversing unbounded collections. Customer report
  history independently retains the newest 100 safe versions and locks preview if any version identity/status
  fails projection.
- The limit disclosure is now a shared design-system primitive reused by Intelligence and Report Studio. Each
  affected workspace states the service total and browser-safe shown count and says that the full collection
  requires service pagination or controlled export. Required fields that fail DLP/type/time/hash validation are
  tracked separately from ordinary truncation. Secret-shaped comments are dropped before React state, but cannot
  make the “comments clear” gate pass; invalid facts/sections/bindings also lock approval. Partial sections cannot
  be submitted as a replacement immutable version, and partial actions/retests cannot be treated as the latest
  outcome or receive a new write.
- Removed three report workflow inferences exposed by that audit. Review gates are no longer hard-coded true:
  frozen facts and evidence must exist in a complete safe projection, AI sections require an explicit reviewer
  confirmation, and unresolved comments must be individually included in the pending review. The local comment
  control no longer claims it changed backend `resolved_at`; it is labelled “纳入本次审核”, and the persisted
  approval remains the authoritative server fact. Workflow state now comes from the report's authoritative
  `draft/review/approved/published` state rather than the version status, so a reloaded approved report no longer
  falls back to frozen. Customer and Report Studio report reads also cancel superseded page requests, preventing
  stale browser-history responses from overwriting the current page.
- The OpenAPI `ReportDetail` still returns all nested arrays without cursors or response bounds. S03 has not
  changed the S02 domain/API implementation: this is a contract gap for service-side version/fact/component/
  evidence/comment/action/retest pagination or controlled full export. Until that contract exists, the browser
  uses the explicit safe projection above and fail-closes every write whose correctness depends on a complete
  collection.
- API-client tests pass 17/17, Report Studio tests pass 4/4, shared design-system tests pass 18/18, and all affected
  strict typechecks/architecture guards pass. The new 501-fact, 101-section, 501-binding/comment and 201-action/

## Post-resume audit — round 23 (2026-07-25 21:34)

- The follow-on Intelligence integrity audit found that row-count bounds alone were insufficient. A secret-shaped
  or malformed claim/evidence/source/graph/score/appeal/verdict row below the limit could be silently dropped or
  replaced with a neutral fallback while the browser still treated the remaining projection as complete and
  enabled a verdict, appeal, review or evidence-package write. Page-history and visual-diff rows had the same
  silent integrity loss even though no write depended on them.
- `projectLiveInvestigation` and `projectLiveHistory` now track DLP/type/range/state/ID failures independently from
  ordinary truncation. Every affected workspace exposes an accessible “安全投影不完整” alert and does not claim the
  displayed subset is the full record. Verdict/appeal/review actions fail closed if any score, explanation, Claim,
  evidence relation, source assessment, propagation edge, appeal or verdict projection is truncated or invalid.
  Evidence-package creation fails closed on either a truncated or invalid evidence matrix. Empty Claim, graph and
  history projections preserve the integrity/limit disclosure instead of collapsing directly to an ordinary empty
  state.
- The live score projection is now bounded to one latest score and accepts the OpenAPI/backend decimal
  representation only after a strict 0–1 conversion. Missing or invalid live probability/evidence sufficiency
  displays `—`/“不可用” and locks governance writes; it no longer falls back to the fixture values `0.61` and
  `0.72`. The same correction handles the generated `VisualDiffView.similarity` decimal string, so a real
  `"0.75"` response renders 75.0% rather than “不可用”. The audit also corrected the evidence contradiction
  relation from fixture-era `refutes` to the service's authoritative `contradicts` value.
- Intelligence component coverage now passes 7/7, including a below-limit hostile projection with a valid real
  score/verdict plus a secret evidence rationale and malformed appeal. It proves decimal conversion, explicit
  invalid collections, pending governance, an empty safe appeal ID and absence of all canaries. The new executable

## Post-resume audit — round 24 (2026-07-25 21:46)

- A live-navigation race audit found that Report and Customer had cancellation guards for their recently hardened
  catalog/detail reads, but the Intelligence case effect did not. If a user opened a cursor page and immediately
  used browser back, a slower superseded list or detail/history response could finish after the current request and
  replace the current case's safe Claim, history, score and verdict projections without changing the URL.
- The Intelligence list/detail/history effect now owns a per-run cancellation flag. Cleanup on cursor, page,
  identity context or local-retry change discards the superseded list result before it can update catalog/cursor
  state, and discards a superseded detail/history batch before any projection or verdict state update. It does not
  abort or misreport the request as a network failure, and it never launches old-case detail/history requests when
  the delayed list resolves after cleanup.
- The new executable race holds page two's list response for 700 ms, confirms that request started, returns to page
  one through browser history, then releases the old response. At all three target viewports the URL and page-one
  case remain authoritative, page-two detail request count stays exactly zero, page-one Claim remains visible and
  the stale case/Claim plus response canaries never reach DOM, URL or browser storage. The combined local-retry and
  superseded-response run passes 6/6 in 32,342 ms with WCAG-AA and zero console/failed-request findings:

## Post-resume audit — round 26 (2026-07-25 22:21)

- A cross-application time-truth audit found that several detail projections still trusted JavaScript's broad
  `Date.parse` semantics even though the generated list summaries had just been hardened. In particular,
  `Date.parse("1")` is finite and is interpreted as a date in 2001; prefix-only checks also admitted timestamps
  without a timezone, while impossible dates such as 30 February could be normalized. A runtime response could
  therefore make an account verification, authorization expiry, revocation, evidence capture, report action or
  investigation history entry look like a real signed service time.
- The generated-client boundary now exports one shared `projectSafeIsoTimestamp`. It accepts only a complete
  timezone-qualified ISO value, independently validates month/day/leap-year, clock and offset ranges, rejects
  ambiguous/local/impossible forms and applies the browser-secret filter before returning a string. Report and
  investigation summaries plus evaluation datasets, evaluation runs and model admissions all use that projection;
  the three S03 detail applications import the same helper instead of maintaining separate `Date.parse` rules.
- Customer's account projection now fails atomically when a non-null authorization-expiry or last-verification
  time is invalid; the account is not written to React state or copied into the authorization form. Revocation
  receipts, account events, deliveries, client-profile versions, asset confirmations, evidence-center assets,
  answer evidence and live answer rows likewise reject invalid capture/event times before state. Report Studio
  marks an affected collection incomplete and preserves its write lock. Intelligence drops malformed history rows
  and records the explicit incomplete-projection notice. The S01-owned
  `features/execution/ExecutionControlPlane.tsx` still formats its handwritten response times directly and was not
  modified across the agreed ownership boundary; S01 needs to project or contract those execution timestamps
  before S03 can qualify that business feature against this negative case.
- Direct coverage now proves a valid nanosecond/offset timestamp, rejects `"1"`, a local timestamp, 30 February,
  hour 24 and offset hour 24, and verifies the shared governance projectors fail closed when their only defect is
  the time. Customer tests cover account expiry/verification, revocation, delivery, profile, asset confirmation
  and evidence capture; Report and Intelligence tests cover below-limit `"1"` rows. API-client tests pass 18/18,
  Customer 18/18, Report 4/4 and Intelligence 7/7. The architecture guard freezes the shared helper, its calendar
  validation and reuse in all three owned detail applications, and forbids new local `Date.parse` calls there.
- The exact-source focused matrix passes 30/30 in 60,896 ms at 1600×1100, 1024×768 and 390×844. It includes the
  complete Customer account/authorization/pairing/challenge/revocation flow with an ambiguous-time account event,
  the bounded Report write-lock scenario and an Intelligence summary whose created time is exactly `"1"`:
  `tests/s04-evidence/e2e-results-s03-strict-time-focused-final.json`, SHA-256
  `292a5caa387c27bb979f840a193d13d5a9803980814dca987e3a0d7aadc7674b`.
- The complete repository gate passes against the same source: lint/format, strict TypeScript/mypy, all 18
  frontend task suites, Python 168/168, regenerated OpenAPI/client drift, the strengthened frontend architecture

  `947a9b188ff8996d4879f9d1bd6313c2666ccee7c3982b7101c68b6a433a7d32` and
  `06f6bcdaa60e7d8fdd12200907dd85a7101d350cd407dc89ad9dbbd20ae5cd4e`; the ordered
  `apps`/`packages`/`tests/e2e` TypeScript source-manifest hash is

## Post-resume audit — round 27 (2026-07-25 22:48)

- A Customer Evidence audit found that the `limit=2` answer/asset requests and the answer-relations response were
  trusted as already bounded. A service returning more rows could place an unbounded answer list, evidence table,
  citations, related evidence and history directly into React state; each related evidence row also exposed the
  raw anchor-array length without validating the anchors. The relation response was not bound back to the
  requested `answer_pub_id`, and a slower response from a closed answer could overwrite the currently open
  answer's evidence dialog.
- Customer now has explicit browser projection limits of two answer/asset rows per requested page and 200
  citations, related evidence rows, anchors per evidence item and newest history diffs. Answer rows are projected
  atomically against the current `prj_` identity, optional lineage IDs, strict capture time, safe query/response,
  model/mode/region, safe citation count and tri-state mention fact; a backend `mentioned:null` renders
  “尚未判断” instead of being collapsed into the real negative “未出现”. Both page cursors must be internally
  consistent with `has_more`; stale answer and asset page responses are discarded on navigation.
- Relation projection requires the response `answer_pub_id` to equal the selected answer, validates citation
  identity/ordinal/source/hash, evidence identity/type/hash/source/time and bounded anchor identity/ranges/hashes,
  and validates history identity/time/similarity. Truncation uses the shared accessible limit notice; below-limit
  DLP/type failures use a separate “安全投影不完整” alert, so neither condition is silently relabelled as an ordinary
  complete subset. A monotonically increasing request token plus unmount/context cleanup prevents a closed or
  superseded dialog response from entering state.
- Evidence-center asset pages now carry total/shown/invalid metadata. If the service ignores the page limit,
  returns an invalid row or supplies inconsistent cursor metadata, the generated evidence-package action is
  disabled and the write function independently rechecks the same invariant. It can no longer freeze a package
  from a silently partial set of asset IDs. The OpenAPI `AnswerRelationsView` still embeds unbounded
  citations/evidence/history and each evidence embeds unbounded anchors, with no cursors, response maxima or
  controlled full export. This remains an S02 contract gap; S03 did not alter that domain/API implementation.
- Customer component coverage passes 20/20. It directly proves three-row answer/asset responses, null mention,
  selected-answer isolation, 201 citations/evidence/history/anchors, newest-history retention, invalid source/hash/
  range/time rows, four limit notices and absence of every canary. The new three-viewport E2E additionally proves
  199 safe citations, 200 evidence rows/anchors and 199 history rows, sub-10-second dialog projection, WCAG-AA,

## Post-resume audit — round 28 (2026-07-25 23:40)

- A Customer Monitoring audit found that the overview, confirmed-competitor and four breakdown arrays were trusted
  as already bounded, even though the generated endpoints have neither cursors nor response maxima. A service
  could therefore put an unbounded metric/model/day/region/question collection into React state. Invalid
  dimensions, duplicate keys, impossible days, unsupported metric states and secret-shaped labels could also be
  silently dropped while the remaining rows were still presented as a complete window. The three parallel live
  request groups had no superseded-window isolation, so a slower previous filter could replace the current
  competitor, delta and breakdown facts without changing the URL.
- Monitoring now applies explicit browser projection maxima of four overview metrics, 50 confirmed competitors,
  90 days, 20 models, 50 region/mode pairs and 100 questions. It accepts only the four supported metric identities,
  contract states, safe integer counts, domain-valid rates/ranks, real calendar days, correct public-ID families
  and unique dimensions. Ordinary truncation uses the shared accessible projection-limit notice; below-limit
  DLP/type/domain loss raises a separate “安全投影不完整” alert, so the safe subset is never described as a complete
  statistical window. Every filter/retry effect clears prior facts while loading and owns a cancellation flag;
  overview, auxiliary and all four breakdown batches discard a superseded result before any state or notice
  update.
- The executable negative scenario returns 5 overview rows, 51 competitors, 91 days, 21 models, 51 region/mode
  rows and 101 questions, with a malformed or secret-bearing row inside each browser window. It proves the exact
  safe shown counts, sub-ten-second projection, WCAG-AA, zero document overflow, absence of every canary from DOM,
  URL and storage, and zero console/failed-request findings. Its race case holds the old auxiliary and four
  breakdown responses, changes the model filter, then releases them and proves the current competitor/question/
  delta remain authoritative. The three-viewport focused run passes 6/6 in 37,088 ms:
  `tests/s04-evidence/e2e-results-s03-customer-monitoring-focused-final.json`, SHA-256
  `d8389e2f5b34c8b924f7ab228ca8ff9179ab9eba947582847edfcf4bde74b829`.
- The large Monitoring collections exposed a shared responsive defect rather than an axe exception: on mobile,
  a 650px accessible chart/table could expand a Grid item's intrinsic minimum width, making the document itself
  scroll and leaving the scrollable panel unreachable by keyboard after asynchronous data arrived. Grid panels
  now opt into `min-width: 0`; `TableRegion` and `GeoBarChart` own bounded horizontal scrolling and visible
  keyboard focus; charts are declaratively focusable. A scoped MutationObserver marks only direct-table panels
  that have no dedicated scroll wrapper, including tables mounted after an API response, and removes that
  treatment when the table disappears. Customer Monitoring and Intelligence Calibration mobile overflow checks
  pass independently. Three affected reviewed baselines were refreshed; their SHA-256 values are

## Post-resume audit — round 29 (2026-07-26 00:07)

- A Customer governance-history audit found that client-profile versions and asset-confirmation versions trusted
  `limit=2` as an enforced response bound and validated only that each row carried some `prj_` identifier, not that
  it matched the current validated project. A safe-looking row from another project could therefore reset the
  customer declaration form, become the displayed latest asset confirmation or enter history state. Both pages
  also accepted duplicate/non-descending revisions and a `next_cursor` unrelated to the last displayed revision.
  A slower cursor response could finish after browser back and replace the current page because neither effect had
  a cancellation guard.
- Both generated pages now pass through one governance-history invariant before React state: a two-row browser
  maximum, exact current-project binding, safe public-ID/text/time projection, unique IDs, strictly descending
  positive safe-integer revisions, requested-cursor monotonicity and a response cursor equal to the last fully
  projected row. Ordinary over-limit responses disclose total/shown counts through the shared projection-limit
  component and suppress the untrusted next cursor. Cross-project, malformed, duplicate, out-of-order or
  cursor-inconsistent rows raise the separate accessible “安全投影不完整” alert. The latest-value `limit=1` read is
  independently projected before it can reset either form; an incomplete latest projection is never replaced by
  an older safe row.
- Profile and asset effects now clear previous page facts while loading and discard a superseded page/latest batch
  on project, cursor, retry or browser-history changes. Successful writes are first checked against the exact
  current project and then trigger an authoritative list reread; the live fixture now models the persisted v3
  response instead of pinning the old v2 after the write. This keeps the visible latest version truthful and
  prevents a local optimistic list from exceeding the same two-row page bound.
- Customer component coverage passes 22/22. Direct tests prove ordinary three-row truncation remains distinct from
  a cross-project row, duplicate revision, invalid cursor and secret-bearing atomic version. The executable
  three-viewport negative case returns three profile and asset rows with an invalid cross-project row inside the
